Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I'm really only here because my coworkers and the doctors at the urgent care keep saying I’m way too thin and sick. I honestly don’t see what the big deal is about my weight right now, but they made such a huge fuss about it that I felt like I had to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you intentionally restrict how much you eat because you are afraid of gaining weight or becoming fat, or is your low weight mainly due to a lack of appetite or another medical reason?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do intentionally keep my meals pretty light—mostly just coffee, a banana, or some soup—because I'm terrified of gaining weight and feeling heavy. If I even think about eating something heavier like pizza, my chest gets tight with panic because I'm scared of becoming that sloppy, out-of-shape guy my ex-wife used to complain about.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a strong fear of gaining weight or a very distorted view of your body, even when other people say you are underweight?\n\nPatient: I definitely have a huge fear of gaining weight—if I even think about eating pizza or having dessert with my kids, my chest gets tight and I worry my stomach will just blow up. But I don't think my view is \"distorted\" at all, as staying lean is the only thing I feel like I'm doing right in my life. I honestly think everyone is making a mountain out of a molehill, because to me, I just look fit and maybe a little tired from work.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently, and if so, how much and over what time period?\n\nPatient: I've definitely lost some weight since my divorce and back injury when I started skipping meals, though I don't know the exact number of pounds. My pants are hanging off me now and I've had to punch extra holes in my belt. But instead of being worried, I actually kind of enjoy seeing the numbers on the scale keep dropping.\n\nDoctor: When you look in the mirror or think about your body, do you feel you are overweight or that certain parts of your body are too large, even when others say you are underweight?\n\nPatient: When I look in the mirror, I don't see myself as too thin or sick; I just see a guy who looks fit, if maybe a little tired from work. My biggest fear is letting my weight creep back up because I refuse to go back to being the sloppy, out-of-shape guy my ex used to nag me about. Staying lean is really the only thing that makes me feel like I'm doing something right and staying in control.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing anything to prevent weight gain, such as making yourself vomit, using laxatives or diet pills, fasting for long periods, or exercising excessively?"

No, I don't do any of those things like throwing up or taking pills, and my back injury and fatigue keep me from exercising much anyway. I just restrict my food, usually having only coffee in the morning, maybe a banana during my shift, and a light soup or salad at night. I do that because I really don't want to feel heavy or bloated.

But instead of being worried, I actually kind of enjoy seeing the numbers on the scale keep dropping.\n\nDoctor: When you look in the mirror or think about your body, do you feel you are overweight or that certain parts of your body are too large, even when others say you are underweight?\n\nPatient: When I look in the mirror, I don't see myself as too thin or sick; I just see a guy who looks fit, if maybe a little tired from work. My biggest fear is letting my weight creep back up because I refuse to go back to being the sloppy, out-of-shape guy my ex used to nag me about. Staying lean is really the only thing that makes me feel like I'm doing something right and staying in control.\n\nDoctor: Have you been doing anything to prevent weight gain, such as making yourself vomit, using laxatives or diet pills, fasting for long periods, or exercising excessively?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do any of those things like throwing up or taking pills, and my back injury and fatigue keep me from exercising much anyway. I just restrict my food, usually having only coffee in the morning, maybe a banana during my shift, and a light soup or salad at night. I do that because I really don't want to feel heavy or bloated.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your body is too thin or that you need to gain weight, even when others say you are underweight? Or do you still feel comfortable staying at your current low weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I'm too thin at all, and I definitely don't feel like I need to gain weight. I actually feel very comfortable at this weight, and seeing the numbers on the scale drop makes me think I could probably go even a bit lower and still be fine. Everyone else is just making a mountain out of a molehill.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent changes in your menstrual periods, or if not applicable, any signs of low body weight such as feeling faint, feeling cold all the time, or loss of sexual interest?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm a man, so periods aren't an issue, but I did have a near-fainting episode during my warehouse shift recently and I've been feeling pretty weak and lightheaded. I just figured I was working too hard and was worn out, rather than it having anything to do with my weight."
  }

Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Auditory Hallucinations, Visual Hallucinations, Tactile Hallucinations, Olfactory Hallucinations, Gustatory Hallucinations\n[S021] Disorganized_Thinking_Speech: Symptoms where speech or thought appears scattered and difficult to follow due to a lack of logical or consistent flow. Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished. Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy. Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences. Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger.
